SEC-01 - Forbidden Functions
Audit result: failed | Severity: critical | Reported: 4 issues
Description: Detects dangerous PHP functions that can execute arbitrary code or OS commands.

Scanner suggested fix: Never call a function named by request data. Map the input to a fixed allowlist:
$fn = ['a' => 'handler_a'][ $_GET['action'] ] ?? null;

[SEC-01-001] Status: false-positive
Assigned to:
Finding: Dynamic function call whose name comes from user input ($_GET[…]($…)). An attacker chooses which PHP function runs — arbitrary code execution.
Location: src/core/Classes/Author_Editor.php:694
Source snippet:
  693:             } else {
→ 694:                 $field_value = isset($_POST['authors-' . $key]) ? $sanitize($_POST['authors-' . $key]) : ''; // phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.ValidatedSanitizedInput.InputNotSanitized
  695:             }
Fix / decision notes: No source change required for this reported arbitrary-function-execution claim. Callback selection is code-controlled. A hardcoded allowlist would restrict supported extension callbacks and is intentionally not applied.
Commit / PR:
Verification / evidence: Static data-flow review at HEAD 94b187ac6d46a87b548e0ac4451d574ed9804815. Author_Editor.php:750-761: get_fields() supplies args; sanitize is args[sanitize] or sanitize_text_field. get_fields():353-436 defines callbacks in PHP and exposes authors_editor_fields. POST supplies only the argument, not the callable. Scope is the repository code; external extension implementations were not audited. No runtime test needed for a tracker-only disposition.
Related finding IDs:
Updated: 2026-09-08 | open -> false-positive

SEC-02 - SQL Injection Prevention
Audit result: failed | Severity: critical | Reported: 48 issues
Description: Checks that $wpdb query methods always use $wpdb->prepare() for variable data.

Scanner suggested fix: Always use $wpdb->prepare() with correct placeholders (%d, %s, %f) for any query containing variable data.

[SEC-02-001] Status: fixed
Assigned to:
Finding: Use placeholders and $wpdb->prepare(); found interpolated variable {$postTypes} at "SELECT ID FROM {$wpdb->posts} WHERE post_type IN ({$postTypes}) AND post_status NOT IN ('trash')"
Location: src/modules/multiple-authors/multiple-authors.php:4324
Source snippet:
  4323:             $result = $wpdb->get_results(
→ 4324:                 "SELECT ID FROM {$wpdb->posts} WHERE post_type IN ({$postTypes}) AND post_status NOT IN ('trash')",
  4325:                 ARRAY_N
Fix / decision notes: Replaced direct post-type value interpolation with one %s placeholder per value and wpdb::prepare(). Retained the empty-string lookup for no selected types, trash exclusion, ARRAY_N results, integer IDs, transient lifetime, nonce/capability checks, and JSON response. No supported functionality change intended.
Commit / PR: Uncommitted change on fix/plugin-auditor-report-76bfa902.
Verification / evidence: PHP 8.3 syntax lint passed. Isolated test of the actual handler passed four binding/response/cache scenarios (single type, multiple types, empty selection, and quote/percent/backslash payloads) plus missing nonce, invalid nonce, and denied capability checks. Database and WordPress functions were mocked; live WordPress/database integration and auditor rescan remain pending. Current code: src/modules/multiple-authors/multiple-authors.php:4359-4372. Test harness: work/test-sec-02-001.php in the Codex task workspace.
Related finding IDs:
Updated: 2026-09-08 | open -> fixed

Verification / evidence: get_ppma_author_categories(): IDs from wp_list_pluck(); implode/array_fill builds %d placeholders; prepare receives ...$ids before get_results(). No data values inserted as SQL syntax.
Related finding IDs:
Updated: 2026-09-08 | open -> false-positive
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SEC-03 - Output Escaping (XSS Prevention)
Audit result: failed | Severity: high | Reported: 144 issues
Description: Checks that all output is escaped with esc_html(), esc_attr(), esc_url(), or equivalent before being sent to the browser.

Scanner suggested fix: Escape all output before printing. Use esc_html(), esc_attr(), esc_url(), wp_kses_post(), or absint() depending on context:

echo esc_html( $title );
echo esc_url( $url );
echo esc_attr( $value );

SEC-04 - Nonce Verification
Audit result: failed | Severity: high | Reported: 172 issues
Description: Checks that form submissions and AJAX handlers verify a nonce to prevent CSRF attacks.

Scanner suggested fix: Add nonce verification before processing form data or AJAX requests:

check_ajax_referer( 'my_action', 'nonce' );
// or
if ( ! isset( $_POST['my_nonce'] ) || ! wp_verify_nonce( $_POST['my_nonce'], 'my_action' ) ) {
    wp_die( 'Security check failed.' );
}

SEC-05 - Input Sanitization
Audit result: failed | Severity: high | Reported: 94 issues
Description: Checks that all user input from $_GET, $_POST, and $_REQUEST is sanitized before use.

Scanner suggested fix: Sanitize and unslash all user input before use:

$name  = sanitize_text_field( wp_unslash( $_POST['name'] ) );
$email = sanitize_email( wp_unslash( $_POST['email'] ) );
$url   = esc_url_raw( wp_unslash( $_POST['url'] ) );

SEC-06 - Settings Sanitization
Audit result: failed | Severity: high | Reported: 1 issue
Description: Checks that register_setting() calls include a sanitize_callback to prevent storing unsanitized data in the database.

Scanner suggested fix: Always provide a sanitize_callback when registering settings:

register_setting(
    'my_options_group',
    'my_option',
    [
        'sanitize_callback' => 'sanitize_text_field',
    ]
);

SEC-08 - Privilege Escalation
Audit result: failed | Severity: high | Reported: 5 issues
Description: Detects privilege-escalation sinks driven by user input without authorization: wp_capabilities meta writes, wp_insert_user/wp_update_user with an attacker role, set_role, wp_set_password, and wp_set_auth_cookie/wp_set_current_user from request data.

Scanner suggested fix: Require a capability check and a nonce before any role/auth/option mutation, and allowlist the writable fields:

if ( ! current_user_can( 'edit_users' ) ) { wp_die(); }
check_admin_referer( 'my-action' );

SEC-10 - Direct Database Queries
Audit result: warning | Severity: high | Reported: 51 issues
Description: Detects direct database queries using $wpdb that should use WordPress functions or include caching.

Scanner suggested fix: Always go through the $wpdb abstraction layer — never call mysqli_*, mysql_*, or instantiate PDO directly. For $wpdb queries that are safe but frequent, cache the result:

$results = wp_cache_get( 'my_key' );
if ( false === $results ) {
    $results = $wpdb->get_results( ... );
    wp_cache_set( 'my_key', $results );
}

SEC-16 - Debug Output in Production
Audit result: warning | Severity: medium | Reported: 1 issue
Description: Detects var_dump(), print_r(), phpinfo(), and other debug functions left in production code.

Scanner suggested fix: Remove the print_r() call. If you need debugging, use error_log() to write to the PHP error log, or wrap in a WP_DEBUG check that is false in production.

[SEC-16-001] Status: open
Assigned to:
Finding: print_r() found: prints a human-readable representation of a variable. Debug functions must be removed before distributing a plugin.
Location: src/modules/debug/debug.php:133
Source snippet:
  132:             foreach ($dataList as $key => $data) {
→ 133:                 echo '<li style="border-bottom: 1px solid silver; padding: 5px;">' . esc_html($key) . ' = ' . esc_html(print_r($data, true)) . '</li>';
  134:             }
Fix / decision notes:
Commit / PR:
Verification / evidence:
Related finding IDs:
Updated:

SEC-17 - Use of extract()
Audit result: warning | Severity: medium | Reported: 1 issue
Description: Flags PHP's extract(), which imports array keys into local variables and can silently overwrite existing variables — including from untrusted input.

Scanner suggested fix: Assign the values you need to explicit, named variables instead of extract(). This keeps the origin of each variable clear and prevents accidental (or attacker-controlled) overwrites of existing variables.

[SEC-17-001] Status: vendor-file
Assigned to:
Finding: extract() usage is highly discouraged, due to the complexity and unintended issues it might cause.
Location: lib/vendor/publishpress/wordpress-version-notices/src/Template/TemplateLoader.php:68
Source snippet:
  67:
→ 68:         extract($context);
  69:
Fix / decision notes: Vendor dependency file; excluded from local fixes by user instruction. No future local vendor edits. Prior review (historical, superseded by scope decision):
Commit / PR:
Verification / evidence:
Related finding IDs:
Updated:  | 2026-09-08: open -> vendor-file (user scope decision)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SEC-18 - Server Info Disclosure
Audit result: failed | Severity: low | Reported: 1 issue
Description: Flags functions that reveal server / PHP environment details (phpinfo, php_uname, ini_get_all, get_defined_constants, …). Shipped in a plugin they fingerprint the host and can leak secrets. Distinct from debug-output functions (var_dump/print_r).

Scanner suggested fix: Remove phpversion() from distributed code. If it is genuinely needed, gate it behind a capability check (e.g. current_user_can('manage_options')) and never echo the result to unauthenticated visitors.

[SEC-18-001] Status: open
Assigned to:
Finding: phpversion() found: reveals the PHP version. Exposing server/environment details helps an attacker profile the host and can leak secrets.
Location: publishpress-authors.php:52
Source snippet:
  51: // If the PHP or WP version is not compatible, terminate the plugin execution.
→ 52: $invalid_php_version = version_compare(phpversion(), $min_php_version, '<');
  53: $invalid_wp_version = version_compare($wp_version, $min_wp_version, '<');
Fix / decision notes:
Commit / PR:
Verification / evidence:
Related finding IDs:
Updated:
